### 2026-04-20 - Keep post-FSR DLSS startup-window OFF extension one-shot per churn burst and align planner queue reasoning with safe post-FSR swapchain ownership

- **Motivation**: GTA V Enhanced `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_110604_gta_weirdfgbehavior` on build `0.1.2477` exposed a later no-crash-but-bad-state seam after the earlier native-FSR callback and explicit post-FSR comeback fixes. The first `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback in that same session is structurally healthy: CE preserves the fresh Streamline `scQueue=000001960B1F2680`, reaches `PostSL CONFIRMED rendering via re-entrant Present`, submits two PostSL frames, then the user turns FG back off and non-FG overlay recovery later succeeds. The later `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback is different. It never surfaces `OFF->ON via SetOptions`; it only logs `FG state transition OFF->ON via GetState`, but still already has stronger post-FSR recovery proof: preserved runtime-owned `scQueue=000001960AF2EDB0`, `realECL=00007FFE55F39470`, and planner snapshots that repeatedly show `safeBootstrap=1`. Even so, the session remains stuck for tens of seconds in `startupPhase=churnWindow` / `postSLPending=1` / `postSLConfirmed=0` while the log prints thousands of `Streamline Hook: Deferring OFF signal during startup transition window ... — extended startup window` lines. The user-visible symptom matches that state: GTA never really finishes enabling the later DLSS FG comeback correctly, and the subsequent `all FG off` path then has to recover after a failed/unconfirmed DLSS epoch.

- **Talos comparison that narrowed the seam**:
  1. Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_110500_talosnocrash_noissues` on the same build also performs mixed FSR/DLSS switching and keeps working normally.
  2. Talos still reaches the post-FSR DLSS family with the preserved `scQueue`, ECL-side startup progress, PostSL warm-up, confirmation, and sustained `Post-SL overlay SUBMIT` traffic.
  3. The GTA-only divergence in the new logs is not missing queue ownership proof or missing safe-bootstrap proof; it is that startup-window OFF deferral keeps re-extending its own protection window during the later `GetState`-only comeback instead of converging once per churn burst.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. The live runtime fix from the older `0.1.236x`/`0.2468` slices intended startup-window OFF extension to be bounded by a one-shot latch (`g_StartupWindowOffExtensionPending`), not to be level-triggered forever.
  2. Current code drift re-primed that latch on every later steady-state active runtime update in `ApplyCombinedStreamlineRuntimeState(...)` (`else if (signalUpdate.effectiveActive) { g_StartupWindowOffExtensionPending = true; }`).
  3. On the later GTA `GetState`-only post-FSR comeback, that means every active `GetState` plus the mirrored suppressed `SetOptions(OFF)` churn can immediately re-arm the extension latch again after the previous deferred OFF already spent it. The result is exactly what the trace shows: startup remains stuck in `churnWindow`, PostSL never leaves the half-armed startup family, and the session never converges onto the preserved runtime-owned `scQueue` path even though `safeBootstrap=1` is already true.
  4. The same trace also exposed a derived-doc/planner drift. `FG SNAPSHOT` already reported the stronger proof combination (`hadFSR=1`, `safeBootstrap=1`, preserved runtime-owned `scQueue`), but `FG PLAN` still kept selecting `queueRole=wrapperBootstrap reason=wrapper-bootstrap` only because explicit `SetOptions` provenance was absent. That no longer matched the stronger queue-selection story the live runtime intended to follow once startup stops thrashing.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h` now adds `ShouldPrimeStartupWindowOffExtensionLatch(...)`. The rule is explicit and testable: only a fresh OFF->ON activation edge primes the one-shot OFF-extension latch. Later steady-state active polls do not.
  2. `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp` now uses that helper in `ApplyCombinedStreamlineRuntimeState(...)` instead of re-priming the latch on every active update. Deferred OFF can still extend the startup window once for the current churn burst, but once that burst already spent its one extension CE now logs `extension already consumed for current churn burst` and does not silently keep startup alive forever.
  3. `hook/common/fg_session_state.cpp` now aligns planner queue reasoning with the stronger live recovery proof. If a post-FSR DLSS comeback already has `safeBootstrap=1` and a preserved runtime-owned `swapchainQueue`, the planner no longer selects `wrapper-bootstrap` just because explicit `SetOptions(ON)` provenance is absent; it falls through to the safer `swapchain-queue` reasoning instead.
  4. `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp` now adds `StartupWindowOffExtensionLatchOnlyPrimesOnFreshActiveEdge`, and `tests/test_fg_session_state.cpp` now adds `PostFSRDLSSPrefersSwapchainQueueOverWrapperBootstrapOnceBootstrapIsSafe`.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a GTA-only workaround and not a special carve-out for `GetState` comebacks. The generic invariant is that startup-window OFF extension is a bounded stabilization tool, not a perpetual keepalive. Once the current churn burst already spent its one extension, later steady-state active runtime polls must not silently re-prime that latch or CE can strand otherwise safe post-FSR recoveries in permanent startup churn. Separately, when diagnostics/planner state already knows a post-FSR comeback has both a preserved runtime-owned `scQueue` and a safe bootstrap topology, it should not keep labeling that state as `wrapper-bootstrap`; that hides the real stronger topology that live runtime debugging needs to see.

- **Verification**:
  - Re-checked GTA `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_110604_gta_weirdfgbehavior/hook_debug.log` and confirmed the later bad comeback really does stay in `startupPhase=churnWindow` with `safeBootstrap=1`, preserved runtime-owned `scQueue`, and thousands of repeated `extended startup window` lines.
  - Re-checked Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_110500_talosnocrash_noissues/hook_debug.log` and confirmed the same build does not reproduce that endless extension pattern during its healthy mixed-runtime switching.
  - Ran `python build.py --run-tests --tests-only --skip-updates --gtest-filter="StreamlineRuntimePolicyTest.*:FGSessionStateTest.*"`; the focused policy/planner suite passed.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter="StreamlineRuntimePolicyTest.*:FGSessionStateTest.*"`; the focused binary run passed.
  - Ran `python build.py --verify --skip-updates`; verification passed and `build/verification/latest_summary.txt` reports success on lint, sanitizer regression, unit tests, build, and compile-commands generation.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h`, `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp`, `hook/common/fg_session_state.cpp`, `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp`, `tests/test_fg_session_state.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/regression-testing-and-logging.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next GTA check is that the later `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback in the same broad switching session no longer logs thousands of repeated `extended startup window` lines, instead either converges or logs the new `extension already consumed for current churn burst` messages while the startup window actually winds down. The planner/session logs for that later comeback should also stop reporting `reason=wrapper-bootstrap` once `safeBootstrap=1` and the preserved runtime-owned `scQueue` are already present.

### 2026-04-20 - Let authoritative FFX queue ownership override stale Streamline queue provenance so GTA does not keep native-FSR queues on the ECL detour path

- **Motivation**: GTA `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_021433_gtacrash_dlssfgtofsrfg` on build `0.1.2472` still froze on `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG` even after the dedicated callback-backend and one-copy fallback-copy fixes. The native-FSR callback bridge itself now gets much further than the old failures: after authoritative FFX takeover, GTA spends hundreds of `enabled=0` callbacks on the no-runtime-composition fallback path (`originalPresent=resolvedPresent=null`), then native FSR turns on at `frameID=621-623`, the callback backend initializes on `fmt=87`, and CE logs several successful `DX12: FFX present callback rendered overlay on runtime-owned FSR path` entries for both generated and non-generated frames. But the later dump `GTA5_Enhanced.exe_2026-04-20_02-19-04.dmp` still shows the dedicated `AMD FSR Interpolation Thread` and `AMD FSR Presenter Thread` blocked in `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery`, so the remaining seam is still native-FSR runtime deadlock rather than a revived DLSS/PostSL crash family.

- **Talos comparison that narrowed the seam**:
  1. Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_103301_talosnocrash_dlssfgtofsrfg` on the same build also reaches authoritative FFX takeover, then the live native-FSR callback path, and keeps rendering without crash.
  2. The key divergence is queue hookability before the callback run, not basic callback rendering. Talos captures its FFX-owned queue `000001EB5995C4C0` and explicitly logs `Skipping vtable hook for FG runtime queue ... — preserving FSR timing` before FFX takeover settles.
  3. GTA instead first captures the later FFX-owned queue `000002600B5AA0F0` on a path that still also carries stale Streamline runtime evidence and logs `Hooking authoritative Streamline runtime queue vtable 000002600B5AA0F0 ...` before authoritative FFX takeover finishes. A second global fallback capture after takeover then repeats the same stale-Streamline hookability decision on that same queue.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. The failing seam is not that CE could not bring up the runtime-owned native-FSR callback path. GTA clearly does: backend init succeeds, several callback renders succeed, and the queue is already the runtime-owned FFX queue.
  2. The surviving mismatch is that CE's create-swapchain queue-classification layer still let stale Streamline provenance outrank current FFX ownership on the same non-origGame queue. `CaptureSwapchainQueueFromCreateDevice(...)` computed `authoritativeStreamlineRuntimeQueue` directly from the caller/stack evidence and passed that into `DX12_SetSwapchainQueue(...)` even when the same capture also had authoritative FFX evidence.
  3. `ShouldHookSwapchainQueueVTableForFrameGenerationRuntime(...)` therefore still returned `true` for a queue that native FSR already owned, simply because that queue also looked like a preserved Streamline runtime queue from the earlier mixed-runtime handoff. The code comments already document why this is dangerous: the ECL detour overhead on native/runtime-owned FSR queues can stall the FFX runtime inside `ffxQuery`.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h` now adds `ShouldTreatSwapchainQueueAsAuthoritativeFFXRuntime(...)`, so the current owner of a non-origGame swapchain queue can be reasoned about explicitly instead of being inferred only from the Streamline side.
  2. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now computes FFX and Streamline queue authority separately in `CaptureSwapchainQueueFromCreateDevice(...)`. If FFX already owns the queue, stale Streamline provenance is not allowed to promote that same queue into the Streamline runtime/startup-handoff family.
  3. `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h` now extends `ShouldHookSwapchainQueueVTableForFrameGenerationRuntime(...)` with an `authoritativeFFXRuntimeQueue` input. FFX-owned native/runtime-owned queues now force `false` there even if stale Streamline authority is also present on that queue.
  4. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now logs the mixed-authority override explicitly: `Authoritative FFX ownership overrides stale Streamline runtime queue authority ...` plus `Skipping vtable hook for FFX-owned runtime queue ... despite stale Streamline provenance ...`, so future GTA/Talos traces show when CE intentionally kept a native-FSR queue off the ECL detour path.
  5. `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now adds focused coverage for the two new invariants: authoritative FFX queue authority stays distinct from Streamline runtime authority, and FFX queue ownership overrides stale Streamline hookability on the same non-origGame queue.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a GTA-only workaround and not an anti-Streamline special case. The generic invariant is that mixed-runtime create-swapchain evidence may temporarily contain provenance from multiple FG runtimes, but only the runtime that currently owns the queue may decide how timing-sensitive that queue is. Once FFX/native FSR already owns a runtime queue, stale Streamline evidence from the same mixed handoff must not keep that queue on CE's `ExecuteCommandLists` detour path; otherwise CE can reintroduce exactly the kind of timing interference on native FSR queues that the code comments already call out as able to stall `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery`.

- **Verification so far**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_021433_gtacrash_dlssfgtofsrfg/{hook_debug.log,GTA5_Enhanced.exe_2026-04-20_02-19-04.dmp}`.
  - Confirmed the GTA hook trace now reaches live native-FSR callback rendering before the freeze, which narrowed the seam away from callback initialization and toward queue ownership/hookability.
  - Confirmed via `cdb.exe` that the dump still lands in the old native-FSR deadlock family: `AMD FSR Interpolation Thread` and `AMD FSR Presenter Thread` are blocked in `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery`.
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260420_103301_talosnocrash_dlssfgtofsrfg/hook_debug.log` and confirmed Talos skips vtable hooking on its FFX-owned queue instead of preserving stale Streamline hookability.
  - Ran `python build.py --run-tests --tests-only --skip-updates --gtest-filter="DXGISharedTest.AuthoritativeStreamlineRuntimeQueuesStayHookableWhileGenericRuntimeQueuesDoNot:DXGISharedTest.StreamlineRuntimeQueueAuthorityIsSeparateFromFreshHandoffState:DXGISharedTest.AuthoritativeFFXQueueOverridesStaleStreamlineQueueHookability:DXGISharedTest.AuthoritativeFFXQueueStaysSeparateFromStreamlineRuntimeAuthority"`; the focused DXGI policy suite passed.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h`, `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp`, `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/regression-testing-and-logging.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next GTA check is that `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG` on build `0.1.2473+` no longer logs `Hooking authoritative Streamline runtime queue vtable ...` on the later FFX-owned queue, instead logs the new mixed-authority override / FFX-skip lines when both runtimes leave provenance on the same queue, and keeps the AMD FSR presenter/interpolation threads out of the `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery` stall family. Talos should continue to take the same `Skipping vtable hook for FG runtime queue ... — preserving FSR timing` path and remain stable.

### 2026-04-20 - Keep stale startup-window OFF churn from tearing down an explicit post-FSR DLSS comeback after startup expiry

- **Motivation**: GTA V Enhanced `installed/captureengine/logs/20260419_230437_gtacrash_fsrfgtodlssfg` on build `0.1.2468` still crashed on `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG`, but the failure family was now different from the older Steam / `sl_dlss_g` Present-hook crashes. The first comeback at `23:07:10` is structurally good: CE re-probes `realECL`, preserves the fresh post-FSR Streamline handoff queue `000001DB8D7BDD90`, logs a real `Streamline Hook: FG state transition OFF->ON via SetOptions`, and keeps the startup-handoff plus later decisive synthetic startup Presents on the normal route with `callbackOnNormal=1`. PostSL even reactivates, warms up, and later confirms rendering. The actual crash happens because startup-window `slDLSSGSetOptions(OFF)` churn was still being buffered and then replayed after startup expiry. At `23:07:12.095`, CE logs `transition-cooldown-complete ... runtime=STREAMLINE_NO_FG active=0`, `DX12: Streamline FG OFF — disabled PostSL callback`, releases the preserved `scQueue`, and falls into post-FSR inactive recovery. A later second explicit `OFF->ON via SetOptions` then has to recover without a fresh authoritative `scQueue`, so the first confirmed PostSL submit lands on `origGame` and immediately returns `devRemoved=0x887A002B`, after which GTA shows the visible `ERR_GFX_STATE` dialog and the dump lands in GTA's own message-box/breakpoint path rather than in `sl_dlss_g`.

- **Talos comparison that narrowed the seam**:
  1. Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260419_230820_talosnocrash_fsrfgtodlssfg` on the same build also reaches the post-FSR explicit comeback family: preserved fresh handoff queue `000001A2D2EFFAB0`, `OFF->ON via SetOptions`, normal-route startup Presents with `callbackOnNormal=1`, and later expiry-driven activation.
  2. Talos does not tear the comeback back down at startup expiry. Instead, it keeps the preserved `scQueue`, falls back to that queue in the post-FSR path, logs `origECL=1 realECL=1 sameQueue=1`, passes the three scratch-barrier probes, and sustains `DX12: PostSL post-FSR submit #0..#600` plus `Post-SL overlay SUBMIT` traffic with no device removal.
  3. GTA only diverges after the buffered OFF replay. Once CE has replayed that stale OFF, the recovered epoch no longer has the preserved runtime-owned `scQueue`, so the later direct submit is forced onto `origGame` (`scQueue=0`, `sameQueue=0`) and immediately hits the old `DEVICE_REMOVED` / `ERR_GFX_STATE` family.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. The previous provenance fix from `2026-04-17` was real but incomplete. CE correctly kept the current comeback classified as explicit `SetOptions`-activated even after startup-window OFF churn re-armed the provisional GetState-only suppression latch.
  2. The remaining mismatch was that the stale buffered OFF itself still survived and was replayed later from multiple expiry paths (`GetState`, inline `SetOptions` expiry handling, and `FlushSuppressedSetOptionsOffIfNeeded()`) once the literal startup timer elapsed.
  3. On an explicit post-FSR comeback, that replayed OFF is no longer a trustworthy inactive edge. It is startup/runtime noise from the same comeback epoch. Replaying it after CE has already preserved the recovered `scQueue`, re-probed `realECL`, and switched routing/callback policy onto the explicit post-FSR normal-route family tears down the very comeback CE is in the middle of recovering.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h` now adds `ShouldKeepOffChurnDeferredForHalfArmedExplicitPostFSRComeback(...)`. The rule is: if the current epoch has post-FSR history, the comeback was explicitly activated via `SetOptions`, and PostSL is still startup-pending or active-but-unconfirmed, OFF churn stays deferred even after the literal startup window has expired.
  2. `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h` now also adds `ShouldDropSuppressedOffChurnForExplicitPostFSRComeback(...)`. Once the explicit post-FSR comeback is already the live effective DLSS signal, a previously buffered startup OFF is stale noise and should be discarded instead of replayed later.
  3. `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp` now uses those helpers in all delayed-OFF seams: combined runtime-signal reduction, the `GetState` expiry flush, the inline `SetOptions` expiry flush, and `FlushSuppressedSetOptionsOffIfNeeded()`. New logs now distinguish `Keeping OFF churn deferred after startup window expiry ...` from `Dropping stale suppressed slDLSSGSetOptions(OFF) ...`, so future traces show when CE intentionally protected the live comeback instead of replaying old startup noise.
  4. `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp` now adds focused coverage for: keeping OFF churn deferred while an explicit post-FSR comeback remains half-armed, dropping stale buffered OFF once that comeback is already the active effective signal, and the combined runtime-signal reducer preserving `effectiveActive=true` across the post-expiry half-armed explicit-post-FSR case.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a GTA-only workaround. The generic invariant is that startup-window OFF churn is only trustworthy as a real inactive edge before a stronger recovery epoch has already taken ownership. Once CE has a newer explicit post-FSR `SetOptions(ON)` comeback and that comeback is still half-armed or already the live effective signal, replaying an older buffered OFF into that same epoch reintroduces a false local `FG OFF` transition and can destroy the recovered queue/bootstrap state that the comeback depends on.

- **Verification**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260419_230437_gtacrash_fsrfgtodlssfg/{hook_debug.log,GTA5_Enhanced.exe_FREEZE_2026-04-19_23-07-16_802.dmp,external_93d8df7a-2608-4843-aa92-acfe40e19d9f.dmp}`.
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260419_230820_talosnocrash_fsrfgtodlssfg/hook_debug.log` as the same-build non-crash comparison.
  - Confirmed the GTA dumps are now the game's own `ERR_GFX_STATE` family rather than the old Streamline/Steam hook-chain crash family.
  - Ran `python build.py --run-tests --tests-only --skip-updates --gtest-filter="StreamlineRuntimePolicyTest.*:DXGISharedTest.PostSLOnlyLatchesSuspensionForFullyInactiveSignalDrop"`; the focused policy suite passed.
  - Ran `python build.py --verify --skip-updates`; verification passed and `build/verification/latest_summary.txt` reports success on lint, sanitizer regression, unit tests, build, and compile-commands generation.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h`, `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp`, `hook/apis/streamline_hook.h`, `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/regression-testing-and-logging.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next GTA check is that `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` on build `0.1.2471+` no longer logs any buffered OFF replay that tears the first explicit comeback back to `STREAMLINE_NO_FG`, keeps the preserved `scQueue` alive through startup expiry, and avoids the later `devRemoved=0x887A002B` / `ERR_GFX_STATE` seam. Talos should still retain its existing no-crash `post-FSR submit` family on the same build.

### 2026-04-19 - Use real output color-space detection on the native-FSR callback overlay path so 10-bit SDR targets do not get misclassified as HDR

- **Motivation**: Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260419_221615` on build `0.1.2464` did not crash and did not lose the native-FSR callback path, but the visible overlay looked strangely dim only while FSR FG was active. The session clearly reaches the runtime-owned callback family: `FFX Hook: Installed DX12 overlay present-callback bridge ... originalPresent=00007FF6AC9EBDB0 resolvedPresent=00007FF6AC9EBDB0 usedDefaultPresent=0`, then repeated `DX12: FFX present callback rendered overlay on runtime-owned FSR path` lines on thread `0x2E8C`. The live output format on that path is `fmt=24` (`DXGI_FORMAT_R10G10B10A2_UNORM`). The same session also shows the earlier normal Talos DX12 overlay and the PostSL descriptor-free path rendering on the same `fmt=24` target before FSR without the reported dimness. That pointed away from queue/routing failure and toward a callback-side color/HDR contract mismatch.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. The native-FSR callback path had drifted from the normal DX12 overlay path in how it classified HDR on 10-bit targets. `EnsureOverlayAdapterReadyForFFXPresentCallback(...)` was unconditionally calling `SetHDR(true, fmt=24)` for `DXGI_FORMAT_R10G10B10A2_UNORM`, while the normal `ProcessFrame` path already knew that `R10G10B10A2_UNORM` is ambiguous and only enabled HDR after checking the real DXGI output color space.
  2. On Talos, that means the callback overlay path could PQ-encode overlay colors on a 10-bit SDR output even though the non-callback Talos overlay path on the same target stayed in SDR. A callback-only PQ encode on SDR output matches the exact user-visible symptom of a globally dim or wrong-looking overlay without crashes or lost rendering.
  3. The FFX callback ABI also exposes a premultiplied-alpha extension on `ffxCallbackDescFrameGenerationPresent`, and while the current fix does not change blending behavior yet, the bridge had not been logging that callback-side composition contract at all. Future visual investigations need that evidence alongside the callback HDR decision.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h` now makes the callback/normal-path HDR rule explicit and testable: `ShouldTreatFormatAsDefinitelyHDR(...)`, `ShouldProbeDisplayColorSpaceForHDR(...)`, `IsHDRColorSpace(...)`, and `ResolveActualHDRStateForOverlayTarget(...)` model the intended distinction between FP16, 10-bit UNORM, and the real DXGI output color space.
  2. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now resolves callback-side HDR from the tracked live swapchain/output via `ResolveSwapchainOutputHDRState(...)` instead of assuming `R10G10B10A2_UNORM => HDR`. The callback backend init log now includes `hdr=%d`, and callback-side HDR probing now logs `DX12: FFX present callback HDR check - colorSpace=... isHDR=...`.
  3. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now also logs the FFX callback's premultiplied-alpha contract (`premulAlpha=%d`) for the first few callbacks so future visual regressions can distinguish callback-side composition-mode differences from queue/transport problems.
  4. `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now locks the HDR decision boundary with focused coverage for: FP16 always HDR, 10-bit UNORM requiring display-color-space probing, recognition of HDR vs SDR 10-bit color spaces, and the combined `ResolveActualHDRStateForOverlayTarget(...)` rule.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a Talos-specific branch and not a callback-only hack for one title. The generic rule is that overlay HDR classification must depend on the actual output contract, not only on the DXGI format. `DXGI_FORMAT_R10G10B10A2_UNORM` is used in both HDR10/PQ and 10-bit SDR setups. If one overlay path probes color space and another path guesses from format alone, the same session can render visibly different overlay colors purely because the rendering route changed.

- **Verification**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260419_221615/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log}`.
  - Confirmed the reported Talos seam is on the live native-FSR callback path itself, not a fallback-copy-only path and not a non-rendering seam: `originalPresent=00007FF6AC9EBDB0`, `usedDefaultPresent=0`, repeated `DX12: FFX present callback rendered overlay on runtime-owned FSR path`, and callback backend init on `fmt=24`.
  - Confirmed the same session already used `fmt=24` on the earlier normal Talos overlay path (`[Overlay] Initializing DX12 backend ... fmt=24`, `DX12: PostSL format check — backbuffer=24 psoFmt=24 MATCH`), which narrowed the issue to callback-side color/HDR handling rather than a generic Talos format incompatibility.
  - Ran `python build.py --verify --skip-updates`; verification passed and `build/verification/latest_summary.txt` reports success on lint, sanitizer regression, unit tests, build, and compile-commands generation.

### 2026-04-19 - Add the first shared FG session/planner layer, planner-driven publication, and structured FG session logging

- **Motivation**: `fg-plan.md` called for a single authoritative FG planning layer because the real FG lifecycle was still split across Streamline runtime state, DXGI Present routing/transport, DX12 queue/bootstrap/PostSL logic, FFX callback lifecycle, and shared runtime heuristics. The current tree already had many narrow fixes, but diagnostics and user-visible publication were still decided from multiple partially authoritative seams.

It now acts as a compatibility adapter over the planner-backed session snapshot so the existing transition/replay tests and DX12 transition logging keep the same public contract while the new planner/session layer owns the richer shared state.

  4. `hook/common/overlay_metrics_publisher.{h,cpp}` gained a planner-aware overload, and the main DXGI/DX12 publication call sites now publish visible FG state from the planner-owned `FGActionPlan` instead of each caller re-deriving active/runtime ad hoc.

### 2026-04-17 - Let the startup-overlay resume gate accept a usable same-process foreground window after late startup handoffs

- **Motivation**: GTA V Enhanced `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_024338` on build `0.1.2367` did not crash, but the visible overlay disappeared even before any real FG activation. This was not a "nothing rendered" seam. The early session still cleanly initialized the normal DX12 backend on `origGame`, created the descriptor-free overlay backend, and submitted repeated healthy pre-FG overlay work (`Reinit SUBMIT #1..#100`, no `Present STALLED`, no `DEVICE_REMOVED`). The regression only appeared after a later pre-FG startup-overlay/runtime-owned swapchain handoff at `02:44:17`. CE re-armed startup compatibility, blocked draws while the runtime-owned queue was unstable, then correctly logged `Overlay allowed during startup-overlay compatibility - queue topology stable enough for barrier-free mode` and later `Resuming DX12 overlay after startup overlay windows settled`. But from that point onward the session stayed forever in `DX12: Keeping overlay rendering deferred after startup-overlay resume for GTA5_Enhanced.exe (remaining=0ms)` and never reached the old GTA resource-prime / first-draw-probe / `Startup overlay probe complete - rendering stably` sequence that the previous GTA session `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_020859` did reach after the same kind of late startup handoff.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. The bad session is structurally different from the old native-FSR callback deadlock or post-FSR DLSS comeback families. It stays entirely in pre-FG `STREAMLINE_NO_FG`, keeps submitting healthy normal DX12 overlay work before the later handoff, and never shows crash markers.
  2. The exact failing seam is the post-startup resume gate in `ShouldDelayOverlayInitAfterStartupResumeCompat(...)`, not the descriptor-free draw backend itself. The previous GTA session `20260417_020859` shows the intended countdown (`remaining=100ms -> 6ms`) followed by `Priming DX12 overlay resources before first GTA overlay draw`, `Startup compat resource-prime settle complete`, and `Startup overlay probe complete - rendering stably`. The new session reaches the same `Resuming ...` edge but then stays in the same gate forever with `remaining=0ms`.
  3. The remaining over-strict assumption was that the exact `frameDesc.OutputWindow` handle must continue to be the foreground/usable window during that late resume phase. After Rockstar / EOS startup handoffs, that can be too strict even though a same-process foreground/root-owner window is already stable and usable again. The old early-return branch still returned `true` without any timer budget left, so CE could latch permanently in the resume-defer path on `remaining=0ms`.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/overlay_compat.h` now adds `IsSameProcessWindow(...)` and `IsUsableSameProcessForegroundWindow(...)` so the startup-resume gate can reason about usable same-process foreground windows directly.
  2. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now lets `ShouldDelayOverlayInitAfterStartupResumeCompat(...)` accept a usable same-process foreground window once the normal resume settle timer has already elapsed, instead of requiring the exact original `OutputWindow` handle forever.
  3. The gate now reuses that accepted same-process foreground window as the stable resume window, so the countdown can converge and stand down instead of resetting indefinitely.
  4. `tests/test_fps_limiter.cpp` now adds `OverlayCompatTest.UsableSameProcessForegroundWindowIsAcceptedAfterResumeSettle` alongside the existing startup-resume delay coverage.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a GTA-only exception and not a blanket weakening of startup-overlay safety. The generic rule is: while startup windows or runtime-owned handoffs are still actively unstable, keep the conservative suppression. But once the configured resume settle time has already elapsed, the overlay should be allowed to resume on a same-process foreground window that is clearly usable, even if the exact swapchain HWND changed during the startup overlay / runtime handoff choreography. Otherwise CE can self-latch in a permanent post-startup defer state with no active blocker left.

- **Verification so far**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_024338/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log}`.
  - Confirmed the session still performed healthy pre-FG overlay init and descriptor-free submissions before the later startup handoff.
  - Confirmed the regression boundary is the later startup-resume edge: `Resuming DX12 overlay after startup overlay windows settled`, then endless `Keeping overlay rendering deferred after startup-overlay resume ... (remaining=0ms)` with no follow-on GTA resource-prime / probe-complete sequence.
  - Cross-checked the older healthy GTA session `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_020859/hook_debug.log` and confirmed the intended sequence there: `remaining=100ms -> 6ms`, then `Priming DX12 overlay resources before first GTA overlay draw`, `Startup compat resource-prime settle complete`, and `Startup overlay probe complete - rendering stably`.
  - Ran `python build.py --skip-updates --tests-only --gtest-filter=OverlayCompatTest.PostResumeOverlayDelayAlsoBlocksRuntimeOwnedSwapchainTransitions:OverlayCompatTest.UsableSameProcessForegroundWindowIsAcceptedAfterResumeSettle`; the focused build completed successfully.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter="OverlayCompatTest.PostResumeOverlayDelayAlsoBlocksRuntimeOwnedSwapchainTransitions:OverlayCompatTest.UsableSameProcessForegroundWindowIsAcceptedAfterResumeSettle"`; both focused tests passed.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/overlay_compat.h`, `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp`, `tests/test_fps_limiter.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next check is that GTA `installed/captureengine/logs/...` on build `0.1.2368+` still safely re-arms startup compatibility on the later runtime-owned no-FG handoff, but after the startup windows actually settle it leaves the resume-defer path, reaches the old resource-prime / probe-complete sequence again, and restores the visible pre-FG overlay instead of latching forever on `remaining=0ms`.

### 2026-04-17 - Remove the remaining duplicate fallback copy on the native-FSR present-callback path and log callback provenance

- **Motivation**: GTA V Enhanced `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_020859` on build `0.1.2363` still froze on `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG`. The decisive boundary is not the older `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` `E_ACCESSDENIED` family and not the later post-FSR DLSS comeback family. This session cleanly reaches authoritative FFX takeover (`queue=000001EE8FD916B0`), then hundreds of `FFX Hook: Installed DX12 overlay present-callback bridge ... originalPresent=0000000000000000` lines while native FSR stays explicitly off during runtime-owned teardown, then native FSR turns on at `02:11:05.513-02:11:05.530`, the callback backend initializes on the runtime-owned FSR queue, and CE immediately logs repeated `DX12: FFX present callback rendered overlay on runtime-owned FSR path` entries. The dump `GTA5_Enhanced.exe_2026-04-17_02-11-42.dmp` then again shows the game thread plus the AMD FSR interpolation/presenter threads blocked in `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery`.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. The dump still matches the native/runtime-owned FSR callback family. Thread `1490.3da8` is inside `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery`, and the dedicated `AMD FSR Interpolation Thread` / `AMD FSR Presenter Thread` are blocked in the same module too. This is the same runtime-cooperative FFX seam as `20260417_000253`, not a revived DLSS-side `sl_dlss_g` startup crash.
  2. The session also proves the runtime had no composition callback available on this path: every `ffxConfigure` logged `originalPresent=0000000000000000`, and there was no `Resolved default frame-interpolation present callback` log for `ffxFrameInterpolationUiComposition`. So CE had to rely on its fallback base-frame composition path before rendering the overlay.
  3. The intended no-double-copy rule from the earlier fix had drifted in code. `DX12_RenderOverlayViaFFXPresentCallback(...)` already fallback-copied `currentBackBuffer -> outputSwapChainBuffer` when no runtime callback existed, but `RenderOverlayViaFFXPresentCallback(...)` still unconditionally repeated the same copy whenever `currentBackBuffer != outputSwapChainBuffer`. On this exact `originalPresent=0` GTA path, CE was therefore copying the base frame twice on every native-FSR callback immediately before the runtime threads stalled in `ffxQuery`.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h` now adds `ShouldFallbackCopyFFXPresentSourceToOutput(...)` so the bridge's copy/composition rule is explicit and testable.
  2. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now removes the duplicate helper-side `CopyFFXPresentSourceToOutput(...)` call from `RenderOverlayViaFFXPresentCallback(...)` and leaves fallback composition solely in `DX12_RenderOverlayViaFFXPresentCallback(...)` when no runtime/default callback is available.
  3. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now logs the fallback-copy path explicitly for the first few callbacks, so future traces show when CE had to compose the base frame itself instead of relying on runtime composition.
  4. `hook/apis/ffx_hook.cpp` now logs `resolvedPresent` and `usedDefaultPresent` for each bridged `ffxConfigure(...)`, so future sessions reveal whether the bridge used a game callback, the runtime's default `ffxFrameInterpolationUiComposition` callback, or no callback at all.
  5. `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now adds `FFXPresentCallbackFallbackCopyOnlyRunsWithoutRuntimeCompositionCallback` to lock the one-copy fallback rule.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a GTA-only workaround. A runtime-cooperative FFX present-callback bridge must compose the base frame exactly once per callback. If runtime/default composition is available, CE should render on top of that output. If no runtime callback exists, CE may fallback-copy once and only once. Repeating the copy in both the bridge and the render helper adds unnecessary work on the hot runtime-owned FSR path and can interfere with the runtime's own callback-side synchronization/composition.

- **Verification so far**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_020859/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log,GTA5_Enhanced.exe_2026-04-17_02-11-42.dmp}`.
  - Confirmed the decisive sequence: authoritative FFX takeover on `queue=000001EE8FD916B0`, repeated `originalPresent=0000000000000000` bridge installs, FSR enable at `frameID=429-431`, callback backend init on the runtime-owned FSR queue, then repeated callback renders immediately before the freeze.
  - Confirmed the dump threads `1490.3da8`, `1490.5864`, and `1490.487c` are all blocked in `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery`, matching the native-FSR callback family.
  - Ran `python build.py --skip-updates --tests-only --gtest-filter=DXGISharedTest.FFXPresentCallbackOverlayBackendResetsOnlyForDeviceOrFormatChange:DXGISharedTest.FFXPresentCallbackFallbackCopyOnlyRunsWithoutRuntimeCompositionCallback:DXGISharedTest.RuntimeOwnedNativeFSRSuppressesInjectedOverlayGpuWorkOnlyForFSRStates`; the focused build completed successfully.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter="DXGISharedTest.FFXPresentCallbackOverlayBackendResetsOnlyForDeviceOrFormatChange:DXGISharedTest.FFXPresentCallbackFallbackCopyOnlyRunsWithoutRuntimeCompositionCallback:DXGISharedTest.RuntimeOwnedNativeFSRSuppressesInjectedOverlayGpuWorkOnlyForFSRStates"`; all three focused DXGI tests passed.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h`, `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp`, `hook/apis/ffx_hook.cpp`, `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next check is that GTA `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG` on build `0.1.2364+` still reaches the runtime-owned FSR callback path, but now does so with exactly one fallback-copy step when `resolvedPresent=null`, keeps the AMD FSR presenter/interpolation threads out of the `ffxQuery` stall family, and remains stable long enough to confirm the fix closed the last duplicate-copy seam instead of merely reducing its frequency.

### 2026-04-17 - Preserve explicit post-FSR SetOptions comeback proof across deferred startup-window OFF churn

- **Motivation**: GTA V Enhanced `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_012406` on build `0.1.2359` crashed on the later `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback, which is a different failure family than the earlier native-FSR callback deadlock in `20260417_000253`. The decisive comeback edge is compact: CE preserves the fresh authoritative Streamline handoff queue `0000025B0E88D310`, re-probes `realECL`, logs `DX12: Streamline FG ON after FSR — preserving freshly handed-off Streamline swapchain queue ...`, and then logs the real `Streamline Hook: FG state transition OFF->ON via SetOptions`. But the same startup window immediately begins OFF churn again. At `01:26:14.287`, the session logs `Suppression slDLSSGSetOptions(OFF) during startup transition window ...` followed by `Re-armed persistent GetState-only DLSS FG suppression due to explicit slDLSSGSetOptions disable request`, and from that point the recovered startup family stays on `Keeping decisive synthetic Streamline startup Present on the normal SL route ... callbackOnNormal=0` through `#200` with no `PostSL synthetic startup activation complete` or `PostSL REACTIVATED`. Startup expiry then fires the old direct-callback bookkeeping, but only with `nullptr` swapchains, so activation still never completes. The deferred OFF flush then tears DLSS back off entirely, CE falls into post-FSR inactive recovery, and the first recovered non-FG reinit submit at `01:26:19.499` hits `devRemoved=0x887A002B`.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. This was not another missing-`realECL` / unsafe-bootstrap family. The same session already re-probed `realECL=00007FFC30829470` on the fresh authoritative Streamline handoff before the comeback activation edge, so the old `safeBootstrap=0 because realECL is still missing` explanation from `20260416_215730_talos_fgdisappeared` does not fit.
  2. The surviving mismatch was instead in the meaning of the current comeback's explicit-activation proof. `ApplyCombinedStreamlineRuntimeState(...)` correctly set `g_CurrentComebackActivatedViaExplicitSetOptions=true` on the fresh `OFF->ON via SetOptions` edge. But a later startup-window-suppressed `slDLSSGSetOptions(OFF)` churn edge re-armed `g_BlockGetStateOnlyReactivationUntilExplicitSetOptions`, and `HasExplicitSetOptionsActivationForCurrentComeback()` still required both `!g_BlockGetStateOnlyReactivationUntilExplicitSetOptions` and the explicit-comeback latch. That means startup-window OFF churn could revoke the explicit activation proof for the *same live comeback* even though no real inactive edge had ended that comeback.
  3. Once that trust signal regressed, the shared DXGI startup routing helpers stopped satisfying the post-FSR `callbackOnNormal` gate. The recovered startup family therefore kept the Present itself on the protected normal-route transport path, but never invoked PostSL on that route, so activation remained pending until expiry. The later direct-callback expiry helpers still only had `nullptr` swapchains and therefore could not complete activation either.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h` now adds `ResolveCurrentComebackExplicitSetOptionsActivation(...)` so explicit `SetOptions` provenance for the current comeback survives deferred startup-window OFF churn while the effective DLSS signal remains active, and only resets on a real inactive edge or a later fresh activation edge with different provenance.
  2. `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp` now uses that helper inside `ApplyCombinedStreamlineRuntimeState(...)` instead of clearing the explicit-comeback latch on every non-fresh state update, and `HasExplicitSetOptionsActivationForCurrentComeback()` now returns the explicit-comeback provenance directly rather than also depending on the transient GetState-suppression latch.
  3. `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp` now adds `ExplicitSetOptionsComebackProofPersistsAcrossDeferredStartupOffChurn` to lock the new state-transition rule.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a GTA-only exception. The generic rule is that startup-window OFF churn is transport/runtime noise within a comeback epoch; it must not retroactively change how CE classifies the *provenance* of the current comeback's fresh activation edge. Otherwise the routing layer and the activation layer disagree about the same comeback, which reopens mixed-runtime startup seams even when queue ownership and `realECL` evidence are already correct.

- **Verification**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_012406/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log,external_505db34b-0c2e-4709-bf4e-6323de89d802.dmp}`.
  - Confirmed the comeback really does activate via `OFF->ON via SetOptions` before the later startup-window OFF churn re-arms the provisional GetState-only suppression latch.
  - Confirmed the failing path never reaches `PostSL synthetic startup activation complete` / `PostSL REACTIVATED`; it instead expires startup while still `activationPending=1`, then tears DLSS back off and later hits `DX12: DEVICE REMOVED after reinit submit #1!` during post-FSR inactive recovery.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter="StreamlineRuntimePolicyTest.ExplicitSetOptionsComebackProofPersistsAcrossDeferredStartupOffChurn:StreamlineRuntimePolicyTest.FreshActivationEdgeOnlyTracksOffToOnTransitions"`; both focused tests passed.
  - Ran `python build.py --verify --skip-updates`; verification passed and `build/verification/latest_summary.txt` reports success on lint, sanitizer regression, unit tests, build, and compile-commands generation.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h`, `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp`, `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next check is that GTA `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` on build `0.1.2362+` now keeps `callbackOnNormal=1` throughout the explicit-comeback startup family even if startup-window OFF churn repeats, reaches `PostSL synthetic startup activation complete` / `PostSL REACTIVATED`, and no longer falls through to the later post-FSR inactive-recovery `DEVICE_REMOVED` seam.

### 2026-04-17 - Rebind the native-FSR present-callback overlay backend on authoritative FFX takeover, stop duplicate callback-frame copies, and bound startup-window OFF re-extension

- **Motivation**: GTA V Enhanced `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_000253` on build `0.1.2356` exposed two distinct issues in one mixed-runtime session. The earlier `all FG off -> DLSS FG on` path after stale no-FG cleanup still showed a long startup-churn family: CE seeded startup bootstrap as consumed, GTA later created a fresh authoritative Streamline runtime-owned swapchain mid-startup, and Streamline then repeated `GetState`/`SetOptions(OFF)` churn while CE kept logging `Deferring OFF signal during startup transition window ... — extended startup window` on every OFF poll before the path finally recovered and reached sustained `Post-SL overlay SUBMIT #2800+`. The actual freeze happened later on the user's requested `menu off -> enable FSR FG -> return to 3D` path. At `00:12:18`, FFX loaded and authoritative FFX takeover moved the live swapchain onto `scQueue=0000021A899EE840`. CE correctly suppressed heuristic FSR relatch during the long `frameGenerationEnabled=0` runtime-owned teardown window, then at `00:12:22.306-00:12:22.326` native FSR really turned on and the log reached repeated `DX12: FFX present callback rendered overlay on runtime-owned FSR path` entries. The manual dump `GTA5_Enhanced.exe_2026-04-17_00-13-12.dmp` then showed the AMD FSR presenter/interpolation threads blocked in `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery` immediately after the callback path began.

- **Root cause refinement**:
  1. The final freeze was inside the runtime-cooperative FFX callback path itself, not the old injected queue-submit path. The callback bridge still reused the normal `g_OverlayAdapter`, passed `queue=nullptr` on cold init, and could therefore either fail on a true FSR-only startup or silently keep backend/resources created for the earlier normal/DLSS device across a later authoritative FFX-owned swapchain/device handoff. In `20260417_000253`, that stale-backend hazard is concrete because the later active FSR path runs on the FFX-owned runtime queue and the dump lands inside `ffxQuery` immediately after the callback bridge starts rendering there.
  2. The bridge also still duplicated the base-frame copy into `outputSwapChainBuffer` even when the runtime already provided an original present callback. That is unnecessary work on the exact hot path that now freezes in `ffxQuery`.
  3. The earlier pure-DLSS startup noise came from startup-window OFF deferral being level-triggered for repeated OFF churn. The effective DLSS signal needed to stay stable, but the startup window itself did not need to be re-extended on every later OFF poll once the later authoritative handoff was already established.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h` now adds `ShouldResetFFXPresentCallbackOverlayBackend(...)` so the native-FSR callback path can decide when an existing callback-side backend must be torn down because the live FFX device or RTV format changed.
  2. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now owns a dedicated callback-side overlay lifecycle for native/runtime-owned FSR (`g_FFXPresentOverlayAdapter`, `g_FFXPresentOverlayDevice`, `g_FFXPresentOverlayFormat`). `EnsureOverlayAdapterReadyForFFXPresentCallback(...)` now initializes that adapter against the live runtime-owned swapchain queue instead of `queue=nullptr`, and it resets/rebuilds the callback backend when authoritative FFX takeover changes the live device or format.
  3. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now resets that native-FSR callback backend plus `g_FFXPresentRtvHeap` immediately on authoritative FFX takeover, and releases it again during normal overlay cleanup/shutdown.
  4. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now only performs the fallback `currentBackBuffer -> outputSwapChainBuffer` copy when there is no original runtime present callback to do that composition already, so CE no longer double-copies the base frame on callback paths that already composed it.
  5. `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h` now adds `shouldExtendStartupTransitionWindow` to `CombinedRuntimeSignalUpdate`, and `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp` now gates startup-window extension behind a one-shot `g_StartupWindowOffExtensionPending` latch. Deferred OFF still keeps the effective DLSS signal alive, but the startup window now extends once per churn burst rather than on every repeated OFF poll.
  6. `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp` now locks the new bounded-extension behavior, and `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now adds `FFXPresentCallbackOverlayBackendResetsOnlyForDeviceOrFormatChange`.

- **Why this is generic**: None of these changes are GTA-only. The generic rule is that the runtime-cooperative FFX callback path must own backend state that matches the live FFX device/queue topology, because authoritative FFX takeover can occur after an earlier non-FSR or DLSS epoch on different DX12 objects. Separately, startup-window OFF deferral is meant to stabilize fragile startup churn, not to perpetually self-extend on every repeated OFF poll.

- **Verification so far**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260417_000253/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log,GTA5_Enhanced.exe_2026-04-17_00-13-12.dmp}`.
  - Confirmed the earlier pure-DLSS startup-churn family eventually recovers (`Post-SL overlay SUBMIT #2800+`) and therefore is distinct from the final freeze.
  - Confirmed the actual freeze boundary is the later native-FSR enable: FFX loads at `00:12:18`, authoritative FFX takeover moves to `scQueue=0000021A899EE840`, the long explicit-FSR-off teardown window holds, then `enabled=1` arrives at `00:12:22.306-00:12:22.326` and the callback path starts rendering immediately before the dump.
  - Analyzed the manual dump with `cdb.exe`; the AMD FSR presenter/interpolation threads are blocked inside `amd_fidelityfx_dx12!ffxQuery`, matching the old runtime-owned native-FSR deadlock family rather than the older Steam/`sl_dlss_g` Present-hook crash family.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/streamline_runtime_policy.h`, `hook/apis/streamline_hook.cpp`, `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h`, `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp`, `tests/test_streamline_runtime_policy.cpp`, `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next checks are that the late pure-DLSS startup handoff no longer logs endless repeated `extended startup window` lines for the same OFF churn burst, and that the later native-FSR enable in GTA again reaches the `FFX present callback rendered overlay on runtime-owned FSR path` family without the FFX presenter/interpolation threads stalling in `ffxQuery`.

### 2026-04-16 - Re-probe real D3D12 ECL on fresh authoritative post-FSR Streamline handoff so safe bootstrap can recover after the earlier FG-off teardown

- **Motivation**: Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_215730_talos_fgdisappeared` on build `0.1.2352` no longer crashed on the later `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback, but the overlay still disappeared. The decisive sequence is a no-crash starvation family: CE clears `realECL` earlier at `hook_debug.log:3149` (`DX12: [outer] FG→off — cleared realECL ...`), later captures a fresh authoritative Streamline runtime-owned handoff `queue=000002A3882BA1B0`, suppresses provisional `GetState`, and then reaches a real explicit `Streamline Hook: FG state transition OFF->ON via SetOptions`. After that the session repeatedly logs `Keeping Streamline startup-handoff Present on the normal SL route #1`, `Post-FSR startup-handoff normal-route bypass #1`, then `Keeping decisive synthetic Streamline startup Present on the normal SL route ... callbackOnNormal=0`, `Post-FSR startup normal-route bypass ...`, and later `DX12: ECL hook leaving pending PostSL activation dormant after startup window expiry because post-FSR bootstrap path is still unsafe (activationPending=1 hadFSR=1 safeBootstrap=0 ...)`. There is no `PostSL REACTIVATED`, no `Post-SL overlay SUBMIT`, and no crash.

- **Root cause refinement**: The surviving seam was not transport or routing anymore. Those parts were already doing the right thing: the recovered Presents stayed on the protected normal Streamline route and still used bypass transport. The thing that never recovered was bootstrap evidence. `g_RealD3D12ECL` had been intentionally cleared earlier during FG-off recovery, but the later fresh authoritative Streamline handoff after FSR did not re-probe it. `HookHasSafePostFSRBootstrapPath()` therefore stayed false for the whole comeback even though the session had already re-captured the recovered `scQueue` and later resumed ECL traffic on the new DLSS epoch. That left `callbackOnNormal=0` forever and stranded the visible overlay in startup-pending state.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h` now adds `ShouldReprobeRealD3D12ECLOnFreshAuthoritativeStreamlineHandoff(...)`.
  2. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now calls `ProbeRealD3D12ECL(...)` immediately on a fresh authoritative Streamline runtime-owned handoff after an FSR phase whenever `realECL` is currently missing, and logs the reprobe result or device-query failure.
  3. `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now adds `FreshAuthoritativeStreamlineHandoffAfterFSRReprobesMissingRealECL`.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not another Talos-only branch. Mixed FSR->DLSS sessions can legitimately clear `realECL` during FG-off recovery so stale runtime dispatch does not leak into the next epoch. But once the next epoch already has a fresh authoritative Streamline handoff, CE must re-establish that probe promptly or the existing safe-bootstrap gate will stay falsely unsafe forever. The generic rule is that bootstrap evidence must be refreshed together with authoritative recovered queue ownership.

- **Verification so far**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_215730_talos_fgdisappeared/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log}`.
  - Confirmed the earlier `realECL` clear at `hook_debug.log:3149` and the later decisive comeback edge: preserved fresh handoff `queue=000002A3882BA1B0`, explicit `OFF->ON via SetOptions`, repeated `Post-FSR startup normal-route bypass ...`, `callbackOnNormal=0`, and no `PostSL REACTIVATED` / submit lines through the end of the session.
  - Ran `python build.py --skip-updates --tests-only --gtest-filter=DXGISharedTest.FreshAuthoritativeStreamlineHandoffAfterFSRReprobesMissingRealECL:DXGISharedTest.PostSLActivationWaitsForSafeBootstrapPathAfterFSRPhase`; the focused build completed successfully.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter=DXGISharedTest.FreshAuthoritativeStreamlineHandoffAfterFSRReprobesMissingRealECL:DXGISharedTest.PostSLActivationWaitsForSafeBootstrapPathAfterFSRPhase`; both focused DXGI tests passed.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h`, `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp`, `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next check is that a later Talos `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback now logs the new post-FSR handoff-side `realECL` reprobe, leaves the `safeBootstrap=0` / `callbackOnNormal=0` startup loop, and reaches `PostSL REACTIVATED` plus visible `Post-SL overlay SUBMIT` traffic again.

### 2026-04-16 - Keep the dedicated stale-Steam-hook bypass alive for the later protected post-FSR normal-route Present branches too

- **Motivation**: Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_213438` on build `0.1.2348` crashed during a later `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback after several earlier mixed-runtime transitions in the same session. This was not the just-fixed `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG` `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` `E_ACCESSDENIED` family. The session stays healthy through multiple FSR epochs first, then captures a fresh authoritative Streamline runtime-owned handoff `queue=000001C150E022D0`, suppresses provisional `GetState` reactivation, and later reaches a real explicit `Streamline Hook: FG state transition OFF->ON via SetOptions`. Right after that CE logs `DX12: Streamline FG ON after FSR — preserving freshly handed-off Streamline swapchain queue 000001C150E022D0 during active startup handoff`, then `DetourPresent: Keeping decisive synthetic Streamline startup Present on the normal SL route #1`, and immediately crashes before any `Post-FSR startup normal-route bypass`, `PostSL REACTIVATED`, probe, or submit log appears.

- **Root cause refinement**: The surviving seam is transport again, but one branch later than `20260416_195002`. The earlier fix already kept a dedicated stale-Steam-hook transport-risk signal alive for the first protected post-FSR startup-handoff Present. But the later `keepStartupPresentOnNormalRoute` branch for decisive synthetic startup Presents was still feeding its bypass decision only from the generic `ShouldForceSteamDX12Bypass(...)` signal. That generic Steam helper intentionally goes false once Streamline is already live in `DLSS_FG`, which is correct for ordinary steady-state traffic but too early for this recovered post-FSR startup family. In `20260416_213438`, logical routing was already right: the comeback Present stayed on the normal Streamline route and `callbackOnNormal=1` held. The thing that failed was transport. No bypass line appeared, and both CE's own dump plus the mirrored Streamline dump are again the same stale-Steam-hook null-execute family (`0x0 -> gameoverlayrenderer64!OverlayHookD3D3 -> capture_hook_x64 -> sl_dlss_g`).

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dxgi_shared.h` now adds `ShouldTreatSteamDX12PresentHookChainAsStaleForPostFSRStartupNormalRoute(...)` for the decisive synthetic-startup normal-route branch and `ShouldTreatSteamDX12PresentHookChainAsStaleForPostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRoute(...)` for the later confirmed-standalone normal-route branch.
  2. `hook/common/dxgi_shared.cpp` now computes those dedicated stale-Steam-hook transport-risk signals in both `DetourPresent` and `DetourPresent1` from Steam overlay presence, bypass availability, clean non-wrapper D3D12 entry, post-FSR history, and the respective protected normal-route branch being active.
  3. `ShouldBypassPresentWhileKeepingStreamlineStartupPresentOnNormalRoute(...)` and `ShouldBypassPresentForConfirmedStandaloneStreamlinePresentOnNormalRoute(...)` now consume `staleThirdPartyPresentHookRisk || dedicatedPostFSRNormalRouteRisk`, so the recovered post-FSR swapchain keeps using the bypass trampoline consistently even after DLSS FG has already flipped back on.
  4. `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now adds `SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupNormalRoute` and `SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToPostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRoute` to lock those new transport-risk boundaries.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not another Talos-only branch. The generic rule is that a recovered post-FSR D3D12 swapchain can keep a stale Steam Present-hook chain alive not only on the one-shot startup-handoff Present, but also on the later protected normal-route callbacks that finish startup and sustain confirmed PostSL rendering. Routing and callback policy should stay on the normal Streamline family; only transport needs the bypass trampoline for those protected post-FSR branches.

- **Verification so far**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_213438/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log,crash.log,external_sl-sha-bbeb8b77.dmp,crash_20260416_213515_032_pid17152_tid11976.dmp,external_UEMinidump.dmp}`.
  - Confirmed the decisive comeback edge in `hook_debug.log`: fresh Streamline handoff `queue=000001C150E022D0`, explicit `slDLSSGSetOptions(ON)`, `Keeping decisive synthetic Streamline startup Present on the normal SL route #1`, then no `Post-FSR startup normal-route bypass` line before the crash.
  - Analyzed both the mirrored Streamline dump and CE's own dump with `cdb.exe`; both resolve to the same `0x0 -> gameoverlayrenderer64!OverlayHookD3D3 -> capture_hook_x64 -> sl_dlss_g` family.
  - Ran `python build.py --skip-updates --tests-only --gtest-filter=DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupNormalRoute:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRStartupNormalRouteUsesBypassUntilPostSLSettles:DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToPostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRoute:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport:DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupHandoff:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRStartupHandoffNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport`; the focused build completed successfully.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter=DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupNormalRoute:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRStartupNormalRouteUsesBypassUntilPostSLSettles:DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToPostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRoute:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport:DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupHandoff:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRStartupHandoffNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport`; all six focused DXGI tests passed.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/dxgi_shared.h`, `hook/common/dxgi_shared.cpp`, `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next check is that a later Talos `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback now logs `Post-FSR startup normal-route bypass ...` for the first decisive synthetic-startup normal-route Present in this family, and that later confirmed-standalone post-FSR Presents still keep logging their bypass transport if the session reaches that phase.

### 2026-04-16 - Pass `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` `E_ACCESSDENIED` through unchanged during authoritative FFX takeover instead of tearing down CE overlay state inside the takeover call

- **Motivation**: Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_205811` on build `0.1.2343` crashed on `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG`, but the signature differed from the older Steam / `sl_dlss_g` startup-Present crash family. The session stayed healthy through a long pure-DLSS epoch first (`Post-SL overlay SUBMIT #2738`, stable `runtime=DLSS_FG`, no `Present STALLED`, no device-removed markers). The failing edge only appears when authoritative FFX takeover attempts `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` on the game HWND from `amd_fidelityfx_framegeneration_dx12.dll`: the deep hook logs `E_ACCESSDENIED`, then CE cleans up overlay refs, retries ten times, still returns `E_ACCESSDENIED`, and Talos immediately crashes. CE's own dump plus the mirrored `external_UEMinidump.dmp` are both the same game-side null-read family in `Talos1_Win64_Shipping!src_strerror+0xaad447`, not the old `dxgi!Present+0x5` / Steam / `sl_dlss_g` corruption family.

- **Root cause refinement**: The surviving seam is not Present transport or Streamline startup routing. It is CE's `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` `E_ACCESSDENIED` recovery policy being too invasive during authoritative FFX takeover. The existing guard already bypassed CE cleanup/retry when Streamline itself was managing an active startup or live DLSS swapchain lifecycle, but it explicitly excluded authoritative FFX callers. In this Talos mixed-runtime handoff, that meant CE still ran `CleanupOverlay()` plus retries from inside the FFX takeover call while Streamline was still loaded and the runtime-owned handoff had not settled. That interfered with the runtime-managed swapchain transition and left Talos crashing after the failed handoff.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h` now lets `ShouldPassThroughCreateSwapchainAccessDeniedForStreamline(...)` return true for authoritative FFX caller/stack evidence too when Streamline is loaded.
  2. `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp` now uses that broader runtime-managed pass-through policy in both the deep and inline `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` hooks.
  3. The access-denied logs now distinguish the pass-through reason explicitly (`third-party overlay caller`, `Streamline active`, or `authoritative FFX takeover`) so future traces show which non-interference path was taken.
  4. `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now renames and extends the regression to `CreateSwapchainAccessDeniedPassThroughRequiresRuntimeOwnershipOrAuthoritativeFFXTakeover`, locking the new authoritative-FFX acceptance cases while keeping the old negative cases.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not a Talos-only exception and not a blanket suppression of `E_ACCESSDENIED` recovery. The generic rule is that once create-swapchain authority clearly belongs to a frame-generation runtime, CE must not flush queues, tear down overlay state, or retry the call from inside that runtime-managed handoff. Returning the original `E_ACCESSDENIED` unchanged is the safer behavior because the runtime, not CE, owns the swapchain transition state machine at that boundary.

- **Verification so far**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_205811/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log,crash.log,crash_20260416_205847_819_pid16000_tid11904.dmp,external_UEMinidump.dmp}`.
  - Confirmed the decisive handoff sequence in `hook_debug.log`: long healthy DLSS epoch first, then `DeepHook: E_ACCESSDENIED ... cleaning up overlay refs ... callerFFX=1 stackFFX=1`, ten failed retries, and only then the crash.
  - Analyzed both dumps with `cdb.exe`; both resolve to the same Talos game-side null-read family in `Talos1_Win64_Shipping!src_strerror+0xaad447`, not the older Steam/Streamline Present-hook family.
  - Ran `python build.py --skip-updates --tests-only --gtest-filter=DXGISharedTest.CreateSwapchainAccessDeniedPassThroughRequiresRuntimeOwnershipOrAuthoritativeFFXTakeover`; the focused build completed successfully.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter=DXGISharedTest.CreateSwapchainAccessDeniedPassThroughRequiresRuntimeOwnershipOrAuthoritativeFFXTakeover`; the focused regression test passed.

- **Files changed**: `hook/common/dx12_overlay_policy.h`, `hook/apis/dx12_hook.cpp`, `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp`, `llm-wiki/current.md`, `llm-wiki/frame-generation-switching.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Fresh runtime validation is still required. The next check is that a later Talos `DLSS_FG -> FSR_FG` takeover now logs the new `authoritative FFX takeover` pass-through path instead of `cleaning up overlay refs`, and that FFX either completes takeover cleanly or surfaces its own recoverable fallback without Talos crashing afterward.

### 2026-04-16 - Make one-shot `build.py --verify --skip-updates` the canonical post-change verification path and emit compact verification bundles

- **Motivation**: The existing build/test workflow had become unnecessarily awkward for post-change verification. Agents and maintainers were often mixing focused test-only invocations, full builds, direct `unit_tests.exe` reruns, and ad-hoc log scraping. Two concrete problems in the current `build.py` also made that worse: lint failures only failed the process when `--lint` was the only requested action, and `--jobs` tried to write into `env` before `get_env()` had initialized it. Separately, the top-level `build.log` was still a human-oriented stream rather than a compact verification artifact, and the nested sanitizer regression child reused the same top-level logging path conceptually instead of leaving a dedicated child log inside a verification bundle.

- **Fix**:
  1. `build.py` now supports an explicit `--verify` mode that enables the canonical post-change validation set in one top-level run: lint, unit tests, full build, and sanitizer regression cadence. This is now the preferred workflow after code changes.
  2. Top-level runs now emit a compact verification bundle under `build/verification/<timestamp>_build_<n>/` containing `verification_summary.txt`, `verification_manifest.json`, and a copy of the top-level build log. Stable pointers are maintained as `build/verification/latest_summary.txt`, `latest_manifest.json`, `latest_run_dir.txt`, and `latest_build.log`.
  3. The verification manifest records per-step pass/fail state for toolchain setup, Python tool bootstrap, lint, unit tests, sanitizer regression, build, integration tests when used, and compile-commands generation, plus paths to important artifacts.
  4. The nested sanitizer regression child now writes to its own log file inside the parent verification bundle instead of only contributing opaque child output to the parent run.
  5. `build.py` now correctly fails the full run on lint errors even when lint is only one phase of a larger verification/build flow.
  6. `--jobs` is now applied after environment initialization, fixing the earlier latent `env`-before-init bug in `main()`.
  7. `AGENTS.md`, `llm-wiki/build.py.md`, and `llm-wiki/regression-testing-and-logging.md` now document `python build.py --verify --skip-updates` as the canonical post-change command and `build/verification/latest_summary.txt` as the first file to inspect afterward.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not an agent-only convenience layer. The repo already treats `build.py` as the canonical build entry point, so the right fix is to make that entry point itself produce a durable verification session artifact and a single normal workflow instead of expecting each caller to improvise their own orchestration and log parsing.

- **Verification**:
  - Re-checked `build.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `llm-wiki/build.py.md`, and `llm-wiki/regression-testing-and-logging.md`.
  - Confirmed the new canonical command is `python build.py --verify --skip-updates`.
  - Confirmed the new verification output contract is centered on `build/verification/latest_summary.txt` first, with `latest_manifest.json` and `latest_build.log` as follow-on detail sources.
  - Re-ran the canonical verification path after implementation and confirmed the bundle files were emitted successfully.

- **Files changed**: `build.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `llm-wiki/build.py.md`, `llm-wiki/regression-testing-and-logging.md`, `llm-wiki/log.md`

- **Stale risk**: Medium. The new verification contract is only useful if future `build.py` changes keep the summary/manifest current and preserve `latest_*` outputs. Re-check this page after any future CLI, sanitizer-child, integration, or compile-commands flow change.

### 2026-04-16 - Keep Steam DX12 hook-chain transport risk alive for the first protected post-FSR startup-handoff Present even after DLSS FG is already live

- **Motivation**: Talos `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_195002` on build `0.1.2335` crashed after multiple switching sequences on a later `FSR_FG -> DLSS_FG` comeback. The earlier label fix is still working: the session cleanly logs repeated `DX12: Native FSR explicitly configured FG OFF while runtime-owned swapchain teardown is still active` plus `DX12: Suppressing queue-change FSR FG heuristic ...` during the preceding FSR-off edge. The new crash happens later. CE captures a fresh authoritative Streamline runtime-owned queue `000001376DEA6970`, suppresses provisional `GetState` reactivation, then a real explicit `slDLSSGSetOptions(ON)` arrives. Right after that CE logs `DX12: Streamline FG ON after FSR — preserving freshly handed-off Streamline swapchain queue 000001376DEA6970`, then `DetourPresent: Keeping Streamline startup-handoff Present on the normal SL route #2`, publishes `runtime=DLSS_FG`, and immediately crashes before any `PostSL REACTIVATED`, bootstrap wait, probe, or submit log appears.

- **Root cause refinement**: The surviving boundary is again transport, but narrower than the older unconditional post-FSR bypass rule. The preserved startup-handoff Present was already on the correct logical route. The problem is that the transport-risk signal that decides whether that normal-route Present should still use bypass had become too dependent on the generic Steam startup helper `ShouldForceSteamDX12Bypass(...)`. That helper intentionally goes false once Streamline is actively running in `DLSS_FG`, which is correct for ordinary live startup windows but too early for this exact recovered post-FSR handoff boundary. The dumps prove the stale Steam hook chain is still live there: both `external_sl-sha-bbeb8b77.dmp` and CE's own `crash_20260416_195126_529_pid6988_tid15992.dmp` land in `0x0 -> gameoverlayrenderer64!OverlayHookD3D3 -> capture_hook_x64 -> sl_dlss_g`.

- **Fix**:
  1. `hook/common/dxgi_shared.h` now adds `ShouldTreatSteamDX12PresentHookChainAsStaleForPostFSRStartupHandoff(...)`.
  2. `hook/common/dxgi_shared.cpp` now computes that dedicated risk signal in both `DetourPresent` and `DetourPresent1` from Steam overlay presence plus the protected post-FSR startup-handoff candidate state, independent of whether generic startup bypass would already stand down after DLSS FG becomes live.
  3. `ShouldBypassPresentForPostFSRStartupHandoffPresentOnNormalRoute(...)` now consumes `staleThirdPartyPresentHookRisk || stalePostFSRStartupHandoffPresentHookRisk`, so only the first protected post-FSR startup-handoff Present gets the broader Steam-risk transport rule.
  4. `tests/test_dxgi_shared.cpp` now adds `SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupHandoff` to lock that new decision boundary.

- **Why this is generic**: This is not another Talos-only branch. The generic rule is that a recovered post-FSR startup-handoff Present can still face a stale Steam DX12 Present-hook chain even after Streamline has already re-entered live DLSS FG. That risk is specific to the protected first recovered startup-handoff Present, not to all later post-FSR normal-route traffic, so it needs its own narrow transport-risk signal.

- **Verification**:
  - Re-checked `installed/captureengine/logs/20260416_195002/{session_manifest.txt,hook_debug.log,crash.log,external_sl-sha-bbeb8b77.dmp,crash_20260416_195126_529_pid6988_tid15992.dmp}`.
  - Confirmed the decisive edge in `hook_debug.log`: explicit native-FSR-off suppression still active earlier, later fresh authoritative Streamline handoff to `queue=000001376DEA6970`, explicit `OFF->ON via SetOptions`, then `Keeping Streamline startup-handoff Present on the normal SL route #2` with no `Post-FSR startup-handoff normal-route bypass` line before the crash.
  - Analyzed both dumps with `cdb.exe`; both are the same `SOFTWARE_NX_FAULT_c0000005_gameoverlayrenderer64.dll!Unknown` / `0x0 -> gameoverlayrenderer64!OverlayHookD3D3 -> capture_hook_x64 -> sl_dlss_g` family.
  - Ran `python build.py --skip-updates --tests-only --gtest-filter=DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupHandoff:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRStartupHandoffNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport`; the focused build completed successfully.
  - Ran `tests\unit_tests.exe --gtest_filter=DXGISharedTest.SteamDX12HookRiskExtendsToProtectedPostFSRStartupHandoff:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRStartupHandoffNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport:DXGISharedTest.PostFSRConfirmedStandaloneNormalRouteUsesBypassTransport`; all three focused DXGI tests passed.
